  A.    2017 – 2018 Senior Scholar Athletes

athletes from winter and spring sports.  To achieve this distinction, a student must 
maintain a 3.5 cumulative grade point average while participating in an athletic 
program.  As your name is called, please come forward.

                                  2. D.     02.27.18 
2.       RECOGNITION 
D.  Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an exemplary 
group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the District. This 
evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and secondary 
principals for their exceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy School 
District.  
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college  and career 
ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students and 
act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring that 
parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; and, 
WHEREAS, Troy School District principals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff 
and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise awareness of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:

WHEREAS,  there  may  be  in  and  may,  hereafter  from 
time  to  time,  come  into  the  hands  of  Rick  West, 
Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging  to  or  held  for  the  State,  County,  or  other 
political units of the State or otherwise held according to 
law, and 
WHEREAS,  under  the  laws  of  Michigan,  this  Board  is 
required to provide by Resolution for the deposit of all 
public  monies,  including  tax  monies  coming  into  the 
hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter 
called bank(s) to be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, 
Rick West, is hereby directed to deposit and/or invest all 
public  monies,  including  tax  monies  now  in  or  coming 
into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into

PNC Bank

It was moved by Mr. Hauff and supported by Mr. Miletti 
that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Troy Board of 
Board Recognition Month 
This evening, as part of our Troy School District Mission 
SSAB is made up of outstanding student leaders from all 
four high schools in the District, who come together with 
the expressed mission of leaving the Troy School District 
better  than  it  was  when  they  entered.  They  are 
nominated  by  teachers,  counselors  and  administrators 
and are selected from a group of applicants to serve in 
the Board. 
Yesterday, these students executed another successful 
Martin Luther King, Junior, Day of Service at Athens High 
School,  where  members  of  the  community  came 
together  to  learn  about  the  legacy  of  Dr.  King  and 
perform four service projects that have a direct positive 
impact in his name. 
These students helped to plan and carry out the Day of 
Service as well as a smaller service project each Spring. 
They also give input to Dr. Machesky and Central Office 
S t a f f  f r o m  a  h i g h  s c h o o l  p e r s p e c t i v e  a n d  w o r k  t o  
encourage students at their own schools to participate 
in  these  district‐wide  activities.  Our  SSAB  students  are 
leaders on campus and we are proud of what they have 
accomplished. 
Students in attendance were called by name to come up 
and  receive  a  certificate  and  congratulations  by  the 
Board of Education.   
celebrates  the  work  of  a  very  important  group  of 
individuals: The Board of Education.  
A  l i t t l e  o v e r  t h r e e  y e a r s  a g o ,  t h e  T r o y  S c h o o l  D i s t r i c t  
embarked on a journey to become a World Class School 
District  by  the  year  2020.  That  ambitious  goal  could 
never  become  a  reality  without  the  hard  work  and 
dedication of the outstanding trustees who make up our 
Board. Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board 
members who put students first in all decisions and give 
tirelessly of their time, energy and talents to our school 
community. This community is grateful for all that you 
and all that you give.  
T h i s  e v e n i n g ,  w e  w o u l d  l i k e  t o  r e c o g n i z e  o u r  B o a r d  o f  
Education  members  with  a  gift  that  represents  our 
Journey to World Class. Each Board member received a 
framed print of the TSD’s four characteristics of World 
C l a s s .  T h e s e  c h a r a c t e r i s t ics  were  developed  after 
studying  some  of  the  best  schools  in  the  country  and

 7. B.   02.27.18 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
                                        2018-2019 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary and middle school  buildings’ 
total student enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that 
Barnard, B emis, Schroeder and Boulan Park  Schools be closed to open 
enrollment.  Our goal  for Athens and Troy High  has been to maximize each 
building’s enrollment which has resulted in  both schools being closed to open 
enrollment for the past several years. Based on current enrollment projections 
for the 2018-2019 school year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High be 
closed to open enrollment with the exception of not more than (25) twenty-five 
incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School attendance 
area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Applications for the (25) twenty-
five spots at Athens High School will be accepted during a two-week window at 
the beginning of March. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to close 
Barnard, Bemis, Schroeder and Boulan Park Schools as well as Athens and Troy High 
Schools to open enrollment for the 2018-2019 school year, with the exception of not 
more than (25) twenty-five incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High 
School attendance area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens and Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed 
to open enroll to remain for the 2018-2019 school year.

DATE: February 6, 2018
TO: Dr. Richard Machesky, Superintendent
FROM: Kris Griffor and Mark Dziatczak
SUBJECT:   Schools of Choice Recommendations for the
2018-2019 School Year
The following factors were considered in making a recommendation for a Schools of Choice Program for 2018-2019 
school year:
• Current enrollment across the Troy School District
• Enrollment projections, patterns and trends
• Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district
• Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2018-2019 if the 
decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade students under 
Schools of Choice
• Available elementary classrooms
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:
• Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + low/2) as 
the foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2018-2019 school 
year. 
• Accept SOC applications for 40 Kindergarten, 3 First Grade and 14 Middle School students. Based upon the 
number of applications to kindergarten received for the 2017-2018 school year, we anticipate attracting many more 
than 50 applicants.  This would allow the District to admit 40 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting 
additional students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  
• Middle school students would be placed in a building according to available space.

Education to consider adopting a resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105 
for (40) forty kindergarten and (3) three first grade nonresident students who 
attend a school district within Oakland County  (total students includ e non-
Oakland County residents – Section 105c).

Subject: Fiber Maintenance (Bid #9851) Recommendation Letter 
Beth, 
We received, opened and publicly read bids and proposals for maintenance of the District’s fiber cabling 
at 2:30 P.M. on January 8, 2018. The following is a summary of the evaluation and our recommendation. 
Three proposals were received: Amcomm, Fiber Link and Turnkey Network Solutions.  Several voluntary 
alternates were provided by Fiber Link.  The initial results were tabulated as follows: 
Proposal Responses 
Bidder  Base Bid  
Amcomm  $ 75,791.00  
Fiber Link, Inc.  $ 45,865.00 
Turnkey Network Solutions  $ 29,651.62  
All proposals were evaluated for compliance with the RFP requirements, pricing, technical solution and 
acceptance of terms and conditions of the RFP. All Bidders provided compliant proposals and any 
exceptions or clarifications by the bidders were deemed acceptable.  While all bids were compliant, 
there were variations in interpretations of the bid items.  As a result, we requested clarification from 
several of the vendors to ensure an accurate comparison of unit items and hourly rates. 
At first glance, Turnkey appears to be the lowest bid.  Hiowever, we found the following to be true: 
 Turnkey under estimated the amount of work involved 
 Turnkey’s hourly rates are higher 
 Based on a time and material contract, their higher rates would use up the allocated amounts 
quicker than Fiber Link’s rates 
 Fiber Link’s lower hourly rates will allow the District to accomplish more and to mazimize the 
refunds available to the Ditrct under E-rate 
Based on our evaluation, we recommend the award of the Fiber Maintenance Contract Bid #9851 to 
Fiber Link, Inc.  We are also recommending the District accept Voluntary Alternate 1 and 2 as proposed 
122
by Fiber Link, Inc.  These Voluntary Alternates will provide accurate documentation of the fiber routing, 
inside and outside of the buildings. 
Fiber Link, Inc. 
Base Bid   $ 45,865.00 
Voluntary Alternate #1  $   7,500.00 Provide CAD prints of the outside fiber cable routes 
Voluntary Alternate #3  $   2,500.00 Provide CAD prints of the building fiber cable routes 
Total Award   $ 55,865.00 
Fiber Link, Inc. is the contractor currently providing fiber maintenance for the district and has already 
amassed a significant knowledge of the District’s network.  Additionally, they have proven to be 
responsive and their work is of a high quality. 
It is important to understand that this is primarily a time and material contract. Fiber Link provides this 
service for the District today on an as needed basis.  We have put this out for bid because the service 
has now become eligible for reimbursement under E-rate Category 1.  The District will receive a 
reimbursement of approximately 40%  of their expenditures up to 40% of this approved amount. 
Please contact me with any questions at 810-223-0528. 
Respectfully, 
John C. Foster, RCDD 
President 
Convergent Technology Partners

Re: 2013 Bond Program, Series 2 - Bid Package 21A:  Costello, Martell, Morse and Leonard 
Elementary School architectural components, Troy Union stage, and hollow metal doors at Troy HS,

BP 21A - Contract Award Recommendation – bid clarification 
Dear Mr. West: 
In reviewing the bids for BP21A, the first bidder, Titan Retail Industries was missing an 
allowance noted in the work scopes.  This was reason to disqualify them from award.  The 
second apparent low bidder R&E Development included with their bid a breakdown that was not 
in alignment with the other bids received, even though the overall bid amount was second among 
the bidders.    During the post bid interview with R&E, the estimator attending the interview 
explained that all the work at all of the buildings was included in the base bid total price.  He 
also explained that he had received his hollow metal door pricing late and placed the total door 
cost on the Martell Elementary price line in lieu of breaking up the door pricing for each 
building.  When we asked additional questions about the new wall at Athens and the low price at 
Leonard, he admitted he did not spend much time on the bid form and was rushing while filling 
out the form in an effort to deliver the bid on time.  Through the interview the contractor proved 
knowledgeable of the drawings and had received quotes from vendors that were similar to the 
other bidders and complied with the specifications.   At the end of the interview, the bidder was 
able to provide the necessary information and qualifications to receive a recommendation for 
award.  
The recommendation amount for BP21A is listed below.

desire to attend the National School Boards Association  (NSBA) Annual 
Conference & Exposition, which will be held in San Antonio, TX , from

How to Pay
If your district is NSBA National Connection (NC) or Council of Urban Boards 
of Education (CUBE) district, you pay the reduced fees listed, otherwise pay 
regular fees. Non-NC Canadian districts pay regular fees. To qualify  
for the early registration discount, you must register and pay by  
January 10, 2018.
NSBA events are open to U.S. school districts that hold membership 
in their state school boards association. NSBA reserves the right to 
cancel the registration of any board member or employee of a school 
district not a member in good standing of their state association. In 
the event of cancellation, NSBA is not liable for hotel, airfare, or  
other charges incurred by that registrant.

Dear ,
The following information is in regard to the 2018 MASB Board of Directors
Election. Ballots will be emailed tomorrow to Regions holding elections.
This year, seven (7) seats are up for election; one candidate is unopposed
and a Region and Group has no candidates, thus we’ll only distribute ballots for
Regions 1, 5, 7 and 8.
Each district will receive ONE ballot, which will be emailed to the superintendent
secretary on record with MASB (or superintendent/board president in the
absence of a secretary). Please do NOT forward this ballot to anyone else or
unsubscribe to these messages.
The candidates are:
Region 1 (three-year term)
*Brad Baltensperger—Houghton-Portage Township
Ted Maier—Engadine Consolidated Schools
Kurt Perron—Brimley Area Public Schools

A seat in Region 2 is unopposed—again, no ballots will be sent:
Region 2 will continue to be represented by Mark McKulsky—Iosco
RESA (three-year term)
Electronic voting is a two-step process:
Step 1 = Submit ballot.
Step 2 = Confirm ballot has been submitted.
Unofficial election results will be available on the voicemail recording at
517.327.5915 as soon as possible, but generally after 2 p.m. on the deadline
date. Official results will be approved at the March 16, 2018, Board of
Directors’ meeting, which newly elected directors should plan to attend.
Ballot deadline is 1 p.m. on Wednesday, March 7, 2018.
No local district/ISD funds can be used to campaign for anyone running for
a seat.
Questions? Contact me at 517.327.5915 or chuffman@masb.org.
